# W5 headroom

Fixed policies on W4's 18-room ring. Every `period` decisions, one of the 14 learnable rooms is given a fresh draw of its own process. T14 is the mean of W14 at decisions L/8, 2L/8, …, L. W14 is bits/symbol of the frozen bank on a fresh EVAL=2048 continuation of the 14 learnable rooms, from a zero state, with that room's channel embedding, on a copy of the world at that checkpoint. The final W14 is the checkpoint at L. Lower is better. The anchor is rand_dwell_s with period inf, the same life as W4, scored with the eight checkpoints.

Every policy sleeps for the fixed share 0.20, in visits of 8, placed as in M7b. At L = 2048 that is 408 decisions, share 0.199219.

## Protocol

- World5(world_seed, period) is World4(world_seed). Room names, ring order, noise rooms and Z are unchanged. period inf does not swap.
- After decision t, for every positive multiple t = period, 2·period, … that is at most L, one learnable room is drawn uniformly from the 14 in identity order with default_rng(world_seed + 80000). Its source becomes the same process in h20_world.World(world_seed + 2000 + k), from that world's beginning. k counts swaps from 0. Sleep and corridor decisions are on the same clock. The swap is logged as the decision, the room and k.
- A checkpoint at the same decision scores the world after that swap. The score is w4.evaluate_w14: a copy of the cortex is not stepped, and a copy of the world supplies EVAL=2048 symbols per learnable room. The live life then continues.
- The cortex, stores, coin, sleep dose and sleep placement are W4's, which are M7's bank with 19 channel rows and M7b's fixed sleep share 0.20. Policy rng is default_rng(1000 + world_seed). rand_dwell_s and oracle14_s move as in W4.
- oracle_fresh_s keeps S, the learnable rooms whose rule has changed since it last arrived there. S starts as all 14. The target is uniform over S in ring order, or over the 14 when S is empty, then the shortest path and 8 stays. The first stay of the visit removes that room from S. A tie on the ring goes left.
- World seeds 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5. L = 2048. Periods 128 and 512 for every policy, plus rand_dwell_s at period inf. CPU. Private Kaggle kernels, one seed each, pool of 4, no GPU, no internet. Nothing retuned.

## Verdict (orchestrator, 14 Nov 2026, UTC+8)

**Precondition (drift bites):** at period 128, rand_dwell_s's T14 with drift minus without is +0.336 on the mean. **MET.**
1. **Knowing where the change is pays:** oracle_fresh_s − rand_dwell_s on T14 at period 128 is −0.253 on the mean. It is ≤ −0.15 on
   5 of 6 seeds (seed 3 is +0.200). **PASS.**
Report only: at period 512, oracle_fresh_s − rand_dwell_s is −0.184. oracle14_s is worse than random dwelling under drift
(+0.159 at period 128). Final W14 is noisy under drift (std up to 0.57) and does not rank the policies the way T14 does. That is why the score
averages over the life.

Checks: with no drift, W5 reproduces W4's glances for 200 decisions and W4's W14 at L = 64 exactly. At L = 2,048 the no-drift
lives match W4 exactly on seeds 1–5. Seed 0 is off by 5e-4, which looks like float nondeterminism on Kaggle and is far below every gap scored.

**Mechanism.** When rules change, the place to learn moves. A swapped room starts near uniform, and a shared cortex does not re-learn
it from general skill alone. It needs visits. Going where the change is pays back 0.25 of drift's 0.34 cost. Allocation headroom
comes from changing learnability, not from the number of rooms (compare W4). This is the first world where the bank cortex
with sleep leaves curiosity something to find.
